LONDON, MAY 18 (AP)-WESTERN EUROPE, ANGERED AND DISAPPOINTED BY THE 
SUMMIT CONFERENCE COLLAPSE, RESOUNDED TODAY WITH NEW CALLS FOR ALLIED 
FIRMNESS AND UNITY TO FACE LOOMING COLD WAR DANGERS. 
NOT SINCE THE HUNGARIAN REVOLT OF 1956 HAD SOVIET PREMIER NIKITA 
KHRUSHCHEV DRAWN SUCH BITTER CRITICISM. BLAME FOR THE BREAKDOWN OF THE 
BIG-FOUR MEETING WAS PLANTED SQUARELY ON THE KREMLIN DOORSTEP.

BUT THERE APPARENTLY WAS LITTLE FEAR AT THIS STAGE THAT A SHOOTING WAR 
IN RUSSIA, THE COMMUNIST PROPAGANDA MACHINE MOVED INTO TOP GEAR IN
